From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n18\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF32.0, F32.1, F32.2, F32.3\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: My mom brought me. She... well, mostly it's because I just can't make myself do anything anymore. I just sit in my room all day. Usually in the exact same clothes. I used to do stuff, like... I'd go walking, or journal, or knit. \n\nI was knitting this scarf with this really thick purple yarn. It was supposed to be for my cousin, but her birthday was back in October. October is such a loud month, you know? Everyone puts out those plastic skeletons on their lawns. The guy across the street has a skeleton dog that lights up neon green and it just sits in the grass. I don't know why dogs need to be skeletons. \n\nBut... um, yeah, I just sit in my room now. When my mom comes in and asks what's wrong or why I'm not doing my hobbies, I just... I barely have the energy to even answer her. Talking just takes too much out of me.\n\nDoctor: Do you still find pleasure in the things you used to enjoy, like walking or knitting?\n\nPatient: Pleasure? Um... no. Like I was saying, I just can't make myself do anything at all. I don't knit or go walking or journal anymore. I just sit in my room. Usually in the exact same clothes as the day before. \n\nI can't even pick up the knitting needles. The metal ones click too much. Click, click... it sounds like insect legs on the hardwood floor. Have you ever listened to a house centipede walk? They have way too many legs. It's not really fair that they get so many and I can't even get out of my room to use my two. If I had a hundred legs I'd probably walk to the mall, but they just use them to hide behind the baseboards. \n\nBut yeah, I don't do any of my hobbies. And then my mom comes in and asks what's wrong with me and why I'm just sitting there, and I just... I don't have the energy to explain about the legs or the yarn. I barely have the energy to answer her at all.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel sad most of the time?\n\nPatient: Sad? Um... I don't know if sad is the right word. It's more like... empty, maybe? Like someone scooped everything out. I used to scoop out pumpkins around Halloween. My mom would always buy these huge ones from the farmer's market, the ones with the really thick stems. They smell so weird inside, kind of earthy and raw, and the seeds get all stuck to your hands. We used to roast the seeds with a little bit of salt. Salt is supposed to keep things away, you know? Like slugs. I poured salt on a slug once when I was little and it just... dissolved. It was really awful. I felt bad for days. But I don't really feel bad like that anymore. I don't really feel much of anything. Just... empty. So, no, not exactly sad. Just... nothing.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way, like you can't do anything and feel empty?\n\nPatient: Um... I think it's been... well, this whole decline really started when I began the community-college program. That was about two years and two months ago. \n\nI remember because the main building there was made of all this really gray concrete. It looked like a giant bunker. Or like a block. Have you ever noticed how many things are just shaped like blocks? When I was little, I had this set of wooden blocks with the alphabet painted on them. But the paint was chipping off, so the 'Q' looked exactly like an 'O'. It's weird how just one tiny little scratch can change a whole letter. If you take away the little tail on the Q, it's just an empty circle. Just a zero. \n\nZero is a weird number because it means nothing, but it still takes up space on the page. You have to draw it to show that nothing is there. Like how a hole is just an empty space, but you still call it a hole. If you dig a hole in the dirt, you're just moving the dirt somewhere else. The dirt doesn't disappear, it just... relocates. There was a lot of dirt behind the college because they were building a new library. The tractors were so loud. \n\nSo... I don't know. The concrete at the college was really gray. Wait, what did you ask me again?\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would enjoy things again if you had the energy to do them?\n\nPatient: Enjoy things? I... I really don't know. Maybe? It's hard to picture having energy. Energy is like... electricity, right? Like what runs through the walls. \n\nSometimes I can hear it humming in the walls if the room is really, really quiet. It sounds like bees. A whole hive of them just vibrating behind the drywall. My uncle used to keep bees. He had these big white boxes out behind his barn, and he wore that funny suit with the net over his face. He said the smoke made them sleepy so they wouldn't sting him. Smoke is funny like that, how it just floats up and disappears into the air. Like when you blow out a candle and that little gray ribbon just twists up and vanishes. I used to love blowing out candles on my birthday cakes. The wax would always drip down onto the frosting. \n\nBut... I don't know if I'd enjoy things. I just feel so... empty, like I said. The humming in the walls doesn't really give me any energy, it just keeps me awake.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost a lot of weight recently?\n\nPatient: Weight? Um... I don't think so. I haven't really been paying attention to the scale. Scales are so strange, aren't they? You just stand on this little square and it tells you a number, like that number is supposed to mean something about who you are. \n\nMy grandmother had this really old scale in her bathroom. It wasn't digital, it had this little dial that would spin around with the red line pointing to the numbers. It made this creaky spring sound when you stepped on it. I used to weigh my cat on it. He was this huge orange tabby named Marmalade. He hated being picked up, so he would always squirm and scratch, and the dial on the scale would just bounce back and forth. You could never get a real number. \n\nHe liked to sleep on top of the refrigerator where it was warm. Refrigerators are always warm on top, even though they're cold inside. It's like they're pushing all the heat out to keep the cold in. I wonder where all that heat goes. Probably just up into the ceiling. \n\nBut no, I haven't noticed if my clothes fit differently or anything. I just wear the same sweatpants anyway.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever thought about not wanting to be alive or wished you could just disappear?\n\nPatient: Disappear? I... I don't know. I haven't really thought about dying or anything like that. But disappearing... sometimes I watch the dust motes floating in the sunlight that comes through my window. Have you ever just watched them? \n\nThey look like tiny little stars just drifting around, totally weightless. They don't have to do anything or be anywhere. They just float. My mom used to tell me dust is mostly made of dead skin cells, which is kind of gross if you think about it too much. Like, we're constantly shedding pieces of ourselves everywhere we go, leaving this invisible trail behind us. Like Hansel and Gretel with the breadcrumbs. Except the birds ate the breadcrumbs, didn't they? Birds are always hungry. There's this pigeon that sits on the windowsill sometimes, and it has this iridescent purple and green ring around its neck. It just bobs its head and looks at me with that one orange eye. It never blinks. \n\nI wonder if birds ever wish they could disappear, or if they're just happy flying away whenever they want. But no... I haven't tried to hurt myself or anything. I just sit and watch the dust.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever thought about not wanting to be alive?\n\nPatient: Not wanting to be alive? No, I... I already said I don't think about dying. Dying is like... it's so final. Like when a TV turns off and there's just that black screen with the little white dot in the middle that shrinks down until it's gone. \n\nMy dad had this really old television in the garage. It was one of those big heavy ones with the wooden sides. He used to sit out there on a folding chair and watch baseball games. I don't understand baseball. There's just so much standing around and spitting in the dirt. But he liked the sound of it, the crack of the bat. Bats are made of ash wood, mostly. Or maple. Maple trees have those seeds that spin like helicopters when they fall. We used to call them whirlybirds when I was in elementary school. We'd gather them up in our pockets and throw them off the top of the slide to watch them spin. The slide was metal, and in the summer it would get so hot it would burn the back of your legs if you wore shorts. I always hated wearing shorts. \n\nBut yeah... I don't want to die. I just... I don't know. I just watch the dust.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever wished you could just disappear without actually wanting to do something to make that happen?\n\nPatient: Disappear... yeah, I said that before. I think about it sometimes. Just... fading out, you know? Like how the colors in a photograph fade if you leave it in the sun too long. My mom had this picture of her and my dad from their honeymoon in Florida, and it sat on the windowsill in the kitchen for years. \n\nFlorida has all those oranges. They sell them in those red mesh bags on the side of the highway. The mesh bags always remind me of the nets we used to use to catch minnows in the creek behind my house. We'd wade in with our rubber boots on, and the water was always freezing cold, even in July. Minnows are so fast, they just dart around like little silver flashes. You think you have one, and then you lift the net and there's just wet leaves and mud. Mud smells so specific, doesn't it? Earthy and kind of... metallic. \n\nBut I wouldn't do anything to make it happen. Fading away, I mean. I don't want to do anything. I just... think about the photograph getting lighter and lighter until it's just white paper.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that your thoughts are slower than they used to be?\n\nPatient: Slower? I... no, I haven't really noticed them being slow. They just kind of go. Sometimes I watch the clock in the kitchen, the one above the stove, and the second hand moves so jerky. Tick, tick, tick. It's not smooth at all. I tried to draw a perfect circle once, but my hand kept jerking like that clock. We had to draw circles in art class in high school. My teacher always smelled like peppermint and turpentine. Peppermints are almost too spicy, don't you think? The red and white stripes always remind me of those barber poles. There's a barber shop down the street from my house that still has one of those spinning poles outside. It just spins and spins, but the stripes never actually go anywhere. They just keep wrapping around themselves forever. I wonder who invented those.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your body moves more slowly than it used to?\n\nPatient: Moves slowly? No, I haven't really noticed my body doing that. I just walk the same. Walking is weird if you actually think about it too much, though, how you just fall forward and catch yourself over and over. I watched a caterpillar on the sidewalk yesterday and it moved so slow, just rippling its whole body along the concrete. It was one of those fuzzy orange and black ones. Woolly bears, I think they're called. People say you can tell how bad the winter is going to be by how wide the brown band is on their back. \n\nSnow is so quiet when it falls. It just blankets everything and mutes all the cars on the street. I used to try to catch snowflakes on my tongue when I was younger, but they just taste like cold air. Air doesn't really have a flavor unless you're near the ocean. The ocean is too loud, though. The waves just keep crashing and crashing and they never stop to take a breath. It makes my ears ring if I listen to it too long. But no, my body moves fine.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any change in how your clothes fit?\n\nPatient: My clothes? Um... no, I don't think so. They fit the same. I mostly just wear my green sweater anyway. It's getting those little fuzzballs all over the sleeves, you know? Pilling, I think it's called. I like to sit and pull them off one by one. It feels a lot like peeling a sunburn. I got a really bad sunburn at the community pool once when I was younger. The water there always smelled so strongly of chlorine and... pennies. \n\nPennies are practically worthless now, aren't they? I saw one on the sidewalk by the bus stop yesterday and it was tails up, so I definitely didn't pick it up. Bad luck. I wonder who decided that heads was good luck and tails was bad. It's just a piece of copper. Well, zinc mostly, I think. Zinc is in sunscreen. The thick white kind that never rubs in all the way. It makes people look like ghosts walking around on the beach. But yeah, my clothes are just the same.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like it takes you longer to think of words or respond to questions than it used to?\n\nPatient: Longer to think of words? I don't... I don't know if it takes longer. Sometimes the words are just there, like they're waiting in a line. But lines are always so boring to stand in. I stood in line at the grocery store for what felt like hours the other day just to buy a carton of milk. The lady in front of me had this huge cart full of canned soup. Mostly tomato soup. \n\nTomato soup always makes me think of grilled cheese sandwiches, but only if the cheese is really melted and gooey. If it's cold, it just tastes like plastic. Plastic is everywhere now, isn't it? Even in the ocean. I saw a documentary about sea turtles eating plastic bags because they think they're jellyfish. Jellyfish are so strange, they don't even have brains but they know how to sting you. I got stung by a bee once when I was little, right on the bottom of my foot. My foot swelled up so big it looked like a balloon. Balloons always pop eventually, though. It's kind of sad when you think about it. But words... no, I think the words come out fine.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're moving slower physically than you used to?\n\nPatient: Slower physically? No, I didn't think so. I mean, you just asked me that, didn't you? About moving slow? I still just walk the same. Walking is really just... it's just putting one foot in front of the other. Like those little wind-up toys. I had a little wind-up tin robot when I was a kid. You'd turn the key in its back and it would march across the kitchen table until it fell right off the edge. \n\nGravity is weird like that. It just pulls everything down, no matter what. Even apples. Isaac Newton and the apple, right? But what if the apple was rotten? Do rotten apples fall faster? They get all squishy and brown and the fruit flies come. Fruit flies are so annoying, they just hover around in these tiny little clouds. Clouds look so soft, but they're just water vapor. You can't even touch them. If you tried to sit on a cloud, you'd just fall right through. Like the robot off the table. But no, I don't feel like I'm moving slow.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your thoughts and movements are slowed down at the same time?\n\nPatient: Both at the same time? Um... no, I don't think they do that. My brain just goes, and my body just goes. It's not like they're tied together or anything. Like... like a three-legged race. Did you ever do those in middle school field days? You tie your leg to someone else's leg with a bandana and try to run, but you always end up falling in the grass. We used these bright red bandanas. Red is supposed to make bulls angry, but I read on the internet once that bulls are actually colorblind. They just get mad at the cape flapping around. I'd be mad too if someone was waving a big piece of fabric right in my face. Fabric stores always smell so dusty, like old curtains. My grandma used to have these heavy velvet curtains that blocked out all the light. It made her living room look like a cave. Bats live in caves. They sleep upside down, which looks like it would give you a terrible headache. The blood just rushing to your head all day. But no, my thoughts and my moving, they don't slow down together like that.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like it's an effort to initiate movements or actions?\n\nPatient: An effort? Yeah... mostly I just can't make myself do anything anymore. I just sit in my room all day with the exact same clothes on. I don't even knit or journal or go walking like I used to. I used to knit these really long scarves. The yarn was this chunky blue kind, but it was acrylic because real wool makes my neck break out in a rash. Sheep look so soft from far away, but their wool is actually really scratchy. And they have those weird rectangular pupils. I saw a picture of a sheep's eye on the internet once and it looked just like a goat's eye. Goats will eat anything, even tin cans, they say. Tin cans are so sharp when you open them. I cut my finger on a peach can once and it bled all over the kitchen counter. Blood is so bright red when it first comes out, almost like paint. Paint takes forever to dry if you put it on too thick. But yeah, when my mom comes in and asks what's wrong with me, I barely even have the energy to answer her. It's just... talking takes too much air. Air is invisible but you can feel it when the wind blows. The wind knocked down a huge oak tree in our neighborhood last year. It just snapped right in half like a dry little twig. Twigs are good for starting campfires, if you have matches. But I don't really do anything anymore.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any days recently where you felt more interested in doing things, even if it was just for a little while?\n\nPatient: More interested? No, not really. I don't think I've had any days like that. Everything just feels... flat. Like a deflated balloon. We had balloons at my 16th birthday party, those shiny foil ones that float for weeks. They get all crinkly when they start losing helium, and they just sort of hover near the ceiling fan. Ceiling fans always make me nervous, like one of the blades is just gonna fly off and hit someone in the head. I saw a video once where a fan fell right into a bowl of soup on a dinner table. Soup is good when it's cold outside, especially tomato soup with grilled cheese. The cheese has to be really melty though, like the American kind that comes in those little plastic wrappers. Plastic is so bad for the ocean. All the sea turtles get stuck in those soda can rings. Turtles live for such a long time, sometimes over a hundred years. Imagine being alive for a hundred years. You'd see so much stuff change. But no, I haven't really felt interested in doing anything.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're unable to experience any kind of pleasure, even from things that you used to enjoy just a little bit?\n\nPatient: Pleasure? Um... no. Not even a little bit. Like I was saying before, I mostly just can't make myself do anything anymore. I just sit in my room with the exact same clothes on. I don't knit or journal or go walking like I used to. Walking is supposed to be really good for your heart, like cardio. The humming in the walls doesn't really give me any energy, it just keeps me awake.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost a lot of weight recently?\n\nPatient: Weight? Um... I don't think so. I haven't really been paying attention to the scale. Scales are so strange, aren't they? You just stand on this little square and it tells you a number, like that number is supposed to mean something about who you are. \n\nMy grandmother had this really old scale in her bathroom. It wasn't digital, it had this little dial that would spin around with the red line pointing to the numbers. It made this creaky spring sound when you stepped on it. I used to weigh my cat on it. He was this huge orange tabby named Marmalade. He hated being picked up, so he would always squirm and scratch, and the dial on the scale would just bounce back and forth. You could never get a real number. \n\nHe liked to sleep on top of the refrigerator where it was warm. Refrigerators are always warm on top, even though they're cold inside. It's like they're pushing all the heat out to keep the cold in. I wonder where all that heat goes. Probably just up into the ceiling. \n\nBut no, I haven't noticed if my clothes fit differently or anything. I just wear the same sweatpants anyway.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever thought about not wanting to be alive or wished you could just disappear?\n\nPatient: Disappear? I... I don't know. I haven't really thought about dying or anything like that. But disappearing... sometimes I watch the dust motes floating in the sunlight that comes through my window. Have you ever just watched them? \n\nThey look like tiny little stars just drifting around, totally weightless. They don't have to do anything or be anywhere. They just float. My mom used to tell me dust is mostly made of dead skin cells, which is kind of gross if you think about it too much. Like, we're constantly shedding pieces of ourselves everywhere we go, leaving this invisible trail behind us. Like Hansel and Gretel with the breadcrumbs. Except the birds ate the breadcrumbs, didn't they? Birds are always hungry. There's this pigeon that sits on the windowsill sometimes, and it has this iridescent purple and green ring around its neck. It just bobs its head and looks at me with that one orange eye. It never blinks. \n\nI wonder if birds ever wish they could disappear, or if they're just happy flying away whenever they want. But no... I haven't tried to hurt myself or anything. I just sit and watch the dust.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever thought about not wanting to be alive?\n\nPatient: Not wanting to be alive? No, I... I already said I don't think about dying. Dying is like... it's so final. Like when a TV turns off and there's just that black screen with the little white dot in the middle that shrinks down until it's gone. \n\nMy dad had this really old television in the garage. It was one of those big heavy ones with the wooden sides. He used to sit out there on a folding chair and watch baseball games. I don't understand baseball. There's just so much standing around and spitting in the dirt. But he liked the sound of it, the crack of the bat. Bats are made of ash wood, mostly. Or maple. Maple trees have those seeds that spin like helicopters when they fall. We used to call them whirlybirds when I was in elementary school. We'd gather them up in our pockets and throw them off the top of the slide to watch them spin. The slide was metal, and in the summer it would get so hot it would burn the back of your legs if you wore shorts. I always hated wearing shorts. \n\nBut yeah... I don't want to die. I just... I don't know. I just watch the dust.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever wished you could just disappear without actually wanting to do something to make that happen?\n\nPatient: Disappear... yeah, I said that before. I think about it sometimes. Just... fading out, you know? Like how the colors in a photograph fade if you leave it in the sun too long. My mom had this picture of her and my dad from their honeymoon in Florida, and it sat on the windowsill in the kitchen for years. \n\nFlorida has all those oranges. They sell them in those red mesh bags on the side of the highway. The mesh bags always remind me of the nets we used to use to catch minnows in the creek behind my house. We'd wade in with our rubber boots on, and the water was always freezing cold, even in July. Minnows are so fast, they just dart around like little silver flashes. You think you have one, and then you lift the net and there's just wet leaves and mud. Mud smells so specific, doesn't it? Earthy and kind of... metallic. \n\nBut I wouldn't do anything to make it happen. Fading away, I mean. I don't want to do anything. I just... think about the photograph getting lighter and lighter until it's just white paper.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that your thoughts are slower than they used to be?\n\nPatient: Slower? I... no, I haven't really noticed them being slow. They just kind of go. Sometimes I watch the clock in the kitchen, the one above the stove, and the second hand moves so jerky. Tick, tick, tick. It's not smooth at all. I tried to draw a perfect circle once, but my hand kept jerking like that clock. We had to draw circles in art class in high school. My teacher always smelled like peppermint and turpentine. Peppermints are almost too spicy, don't you think? The red and white stripes always remind me of those barber poles. There's a barber shop down the street from my house that still has one of those spinning poles outside. It just spins and spins, but the stripes never actually go anywhere. They just keep wrapping around themselves forever. I wonder who invented those.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your body moves more slowly than it used to?\n\nPatient: Moves slowly? No, I haven't really noticed my body doing that. I just walk the same. Walking is weird if you actually think about it too much, though, how you just fall forward and catch yourself over and over. I watched a caterpillar on the sidewalk yesterday and it moved so slow, just rippling its whole body along the concrete. It was one of those fuzzy orange and black ones. Woolly bears, I think they're called. People say you can tell how bad the winter is going to be by how wide the brown band is on their back. \n\nSnow is so quiet when it falls. It just blankets everything and mutes all the cars on the street. I used to try to catch snowflakes on my tongue when I was younger, but they just taste like cold air. Air doesn't really have a flavor unless you're near the ocean. The ocean is too loud, though. The waves just keep crashing and crashing and they never stop to take a breath. It makes my ears ring if I listen to it too long. But no, my body moves fine.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any change in how your clothes fit?\n\nPatient: My clothes? Um... no, I don't think so. They fit the same. I mostly just wear my green sweater anyway. It's getting those little fuzzballs all over the sleeves, you know? Pilling, I think it's called. I like to sit and pull them off one by one. It feels a lot like peeling a sunburn. I got a really bad sunburn at the community pool once when I was younger. The water there always smelled so strongly of chlorine and... pennies. \n\nPennies are practically worthless now, aren't they? I saw one on the sidewalk by the bus stop yesterday and it was tails up, so I definitely didn't pick it up. Bad luck. I wonder who decided that heads was good luck and tails was bad. It's just a piece of copper. Well, zinc mostly, I think. Zinc is in sunscreen. The thick white kind that never rubs in all the way. It makes people look like ghosts walking around on the beach. But yeah, my clothes are just the same.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like it takes you longer to think of words or respond to questions than it used to?\n\nPatient: Longer to think of words? I don't... I don't know if it takes longer. Sometimes the words are just there, like they're waiting in a line. But lines are always so boring to stand in. I stood in line at the grocery store for what felt like hours the other day just to buy a carton of milk. The lady in front of me had this huge cart full of canned soup. Mostly tomato soup. \n\nTomato soup always makes me think of grilled cheese sandwiches, but only if the cheese is really melted and gooey. If it's cold, it just tastes like plastic. Plastic is everywhere now, isn't it? Even in the ocean. I saw a documentary about sea turtles eating plastic bags because they think they're jellyfish. Jellyfish are so strange, they don't even have brains but they know how to sting you. I got stung by a bee once when I was little, right on the bottom of my foot. My foot swelled up so big it looked like a balloon. Balloons always pop eventually, though. It's kind of sad when you think about it. But words... no, I think the words come out fine.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're moving slower physically than you used to?\n\nPatient: Slower physically? No, I didn't think so. I mean, you just asked me that, didn't you? About moving slow? I still just walk the same. Walking is really just... it's just putting one foot in front of the other. Like those little wind-up toys. I had a little wind-up tin robot when I was a kid. You'd turn the key in its back and it would march across the kitchen table until it fell right off the edge. \n\nGravity is weird like that. It just pulls everything down, no matter what. Even apples. Isaac Newton and the apple, right? But what if the apple was rotten? Do rotten apples fall faster? They get all squishy and brown and the fruit flies come. Fruit flies are so annoying, they just hover around in these tiny little clouds. Clouds look so soft, but they're just water vapor. You can't even touch them. If you tried to sit on a cloud, you'd just fall right through. Like the robot off the table. But no, I don't feel like I'm moving slow.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your thoughts and movements are slowed down at the same time?\n\nPatient: Both at the same time? Um... no, I don't think they do that. My brain just goes, and my body just goes. It's not like they're tied together or anything. Like... like a three-legged race. Did you ever do those in middle school field days? You tie your leg to someone else's leg with a bandana and try to run, but you always end up falling in the grass. We used these bright red bandanas. Red is supposed to make bulls angry, but I read on the internet once that bulls are actually colorblind. They just get mad at the cape flapping around. I'd be mad too if someone was waving a big piece of fabric right in my face. Fabric stores always smell so dusty, like old curtains. My grandma used to have these heavy velvet curtains that blocked out all the light. It made her living room look like a cave. Bats live in caves. They sleep upside down, which looks like it would give you a terrible headache. The blood just rushing to your head all day. But no, my thoughts and my moving, they don't slow down together like that.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like it's an effort to initiate movements or actions?\n\nPatient: An effort? Yeah... mostly I just can't make myself do anything anymore. I just sit in my room all day with the exact same clothes on. I don't even knit or journal or go walking like I used to. I used to knit these really long scarves. The yarn was this chunky blue kind, but it was acrylic because real wool makes my neck break out in a rash. Sheep look so soft from far away, but their wool is actually really scratchy. And they have those weird rectangular pupils. I saw a picture of a sheep's eye on the internet once and it looked just like a goat's eye. Goats will eat anything, even tin cans, they say. Tin cans are so sharp when you open them. I cut my finger on a peach can once and it bled all over the kitchen counter. Blood is so bright red when it first comes out, almost like paint. Paint takes forever to dry if you put it on too thick. But yeah, when my mom comes in and asks what's wrong with me, I barely even have the energy to answer her. It's just... talking takes too much air. Air is invisible but you can feel it when the wind blows. The wind knocked down a huge oak tree in our neighborhood last year. It just snapped right in half like a dry little twig. Twigs are good for starting campfires, if you have matches. But I don't really do anything anymore.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any days recently where you felt more interested in doing things, even if it was just for a little while?\n\nPatient: More interested? No, not really. I don't think I've had any days like that. Everything just feels... flat. Like a deflated balloon. We had balloons at my 16th birthday party, those shiny foil ones that float for weeks. They get all crinkly when they start losing helium, and they just sort of hover near the ceiling fan. Ceiling fans always make me nervous, like one of the blades is just gonna fly off and hit someone in the head. I saw a video once where a fan fell right into a bowl of soup on a dinner table. Soup is good when it's cold outside, especially tomato soup with grilled cheese. The cheese has to be really melty though, like the American kind that comes in those little plastic wrappers. Plastic is so bad for the ocean. All the sea turtles get stuck in those soda can rings. Turtles live for such a long time, sometimes over a hundred years. Imagine being alive for a hundred years. You'd see so much stuff change. But no, I haven't really felt interested in doing anything.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're unable to experience any kind of pleasure, even from things that you used to enjoy just a little bit?\n\nPatient: Pleasure? Um... no. Not even a little bit. Like I was saying before, I mostly just can't make myself do anything anymore. I just sit in my room with the exact same clothes on. I don't knit or journal or go walking like I used to. Walking is supposed to be really good for your heart, like cardio. My gym teacher in high school always made us run the mile, and he'd stand there with a stopwatch yelling at us to go faster. Stopwatches have that really loud clicking sound when you press the button. It sounds like a bug snapping its wings. Cicadas make the loudest noise in the summer. They leave those empty brown shells stuck to the sides of trees. Trees have rings inside them that tell you how old they are. If you cut one open, you can count them. But I wouldn't want to cut down a tree, they give us oxygen. But yeah, I don't enjoy anything. When my mom comes in and asks what's wrong, I barely even have the energy to answer her. It's just... nothing feels good anymore.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. The worst? I don't know... I guess so. It's hard to tell what the worst is when everything just feels the same every day. Every day is just a Tuesday. Tuesday is such a weird word when you look at it for too long. T-u-e-s. It doesn't even sound like how it's spelled. English is a really confusing language, I feel bad for people who have to learn it as a second language. My Spanish teacher in high school, Señora Martinez, she told us that Spanish is phonetic so you just say it how it looks. She had this really bright red lipstick she wore every single day. It matched her car, she drove this tiny little red convertible. Convertibles seem so dangerous if it rains suddenly. You'd get completely soaked before you could get the top back up. Rain always smells so specific, like wet dirt and pavement. Pavement gets so hot in the summer you could fry an egg on it. I saw a cartoon where someone did that once, but I don't think it would actually taste very good. The yolk would probably get gravel in it. Gravel driveways are so loud when you drive on them, they go crunch crunch crunch. But yeah... I don't really know if it's the worst. It's just bad.

Usually in the exact same clothes. I used to do stuff, like... I'd go walking, or journal, or knit. \n\nI was knitting this scarf with this really thick purple yarn. It was supposed to be for my cousin, but her birthday was back in October. October is such a loud month, you know? Everyone puts out those plastic skeletons on their lawns. The guy across the street has a skeleton dog that lights up neon green and it just sits in the grass. I don't know why dogs need to be skeletons. \n\nBut... um, yeah, I just sit in my room now. When my mom comes in and asks what's wrong or why I'm not doing my hobbies, I just... I barely have the energy to even answer her. Talking just takes too much out of me.\n\nDoctor: Do you still find pleasure in the things you used to enjoy, like walking or knitting?\n\nPatient: Pleasure? Um... no. Like I was saying, I just can't make myself do anything at all. I don't knit or go walking or journal anymore. I just sit in my room. Usually in the exact same clothes as the day before. \n\nI can't even pick up the knitting needles. The metal ones click too much. Click, click... it sounds like insect legs on the hardwood floor. Have you ever listened to a house centipede walk? They have way too many legs. It's not really fair that they get so many and I can't even get out of my room to use my two. If I had a hundred legs I'd probably walk to the mall, but they just use them to hide behind the baseboards. \n\nBut yeah, I don't do any of my hobbies. And then my mom comes in and asks what's wrong with me and why I'm just sitting there, and I just... I don't have the energy to explain about the legs or the yarn. I barely have the energy to answer her at all.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel sad most of the time?\n\nPatient: Sad? Um... I don't know if sad is the right word. It's more like... empty, maybe? Like someone scooped everything out. I used to scoop out pumpkins around Halloween. My mom would always buy these huge ones from the farmer's market, the ones with the really thick stems. They smell so weird inside, kind of earthy and raw, and the seeds get all stuck to your hands. We used to roast the seeds with a little bit of salt. Salt is supposed to keep things away, you know? Like slugs. I poured salt on a slug once when I was little and it just... dissolved. It was really awful. I felt bad for days. But I don't really feel bad like that anymore. I don't really feel much of anything. Just... empty. So, no, not exactly sad. Just... nothing.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way, like you can't do anything and feel empty?\n\nPatient: Um... I think it's been... well, this whole decline really started when I began the community-college program. That was about two years and two months ago. \n\nI remember because the main building there was made of all this really gray concrete. It looked like a giant bunker. Or like a block. Have you ever noticed how many things are just shaped like blocks? When I was little, I had this set of wooden blocks with the alphabet painted on them. But the paint was chipping off, so the 'Q' looked exactly like an 'O'. It's weird how just one tiny little scratch can change a whole letter. If you take away the little tail on the Q, it's just an empty circle. Just a zero. \n\nZero is a weird number because it means nothing, but it still takes up space on the page. You have to draw it to show that nothing is there. Like how a hole is just an empty space, but you still call it a hole. If you dig a hole in the dirt, you're just moving the dirt somewhere else. The dirt doesn't disappear, it just... relocates. There was a lot of dirt behind the college because they were building a new library. The tractors were so loud. \n\nSo... I don't know. The concrete at the college was really gray. Wait, what did you ask me again?\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would enjoy things again if you had the energy to do them?\n\nPatient: Enjoy things? I... I really don't know. Maybe? It's hard to picture having energy. Energy is like... electricity, right? Like what runs through the walls. \n\nSometimes I can hear it humming in the walls if the room is really, really quiet. It sounds like bees. A whole hive of them just vibrating behind the drywall. My uncle used to keep bees. He had these big white boxes out behind his barn, and he wore that funny suit with the net over his face. He said the smoke made them sleepy so they wouldn't sting him. Smoke is funny like that, how it just floats up and disappears into the air. Like when you blow out a candle and that little gray ribbon just twists up and vanishes. I used to love blowing out candles on my birthday cakes. The wax would always drip down onto the frosting. \n\nBut... I don't know if I'd enjoy things. I just feel so... empty, like I said. The humming in the walls doesn't really give me any energy, it just keeps me awake.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost a lot of weight recently?\n\nPatient: Weight? Um... I don't think so. I haven't really been paying attention to the scale. Scales are so strange, aren't they? You just stand on this little square and it tells you a number, like that number is supposed to mean something about who you are. \n\nMy grandmother had this really old scale in her bathroom. It wasn't digital, it had this little dial that would spin around with the red line pointing to the numbers. It made this creaky spring sound when you stepped on it. I used to weigh my cat on it. He was this huge orange tabby named Marmalade. He hated being picked up, so he would always squirm and scratch, and the dial on the scale would just bounce back and forth. You could never get a real number. \n\nHe liked to sleep on top of the refrigerator where it was warm. Refrigerators are always warm on top, even though they're cold inside. It's like they're pushing all the heat out to keep the cold in. I wonder where all that heat goes. Probably just up into the ceiling. \n\nBut no, I haven't noticed if my clothes fit differently or anything. I just wear the same sweatpants anyway.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever thought about not wanting to be alive or wished you could just disappear?\n\nPatient: Disappear? I... I don't know. I haven't really thought about dying or anything like that. But disappearing... sometimes I watch the dust motes floating in the sunlight that comes through my window. Have you ever just watched them? \n\nThey look like tiny little stars just drifting around, totally weightless. They don't have to do anything or be anywhere. They just float. My mom used to tell me dust is mostly made of dead skin cells, which is kind of gross if you think about it too much. Like, we're constantly shedding pieces of ourselves everywhere we go, leaving this invisible trail behind us. Like Hansel and Gretel with the breadcrumbs. Except the birds ate the breadcrumbs, didn't they? Birds are always hungry. There's this pigeon that sits on the windowsill sometimes, and it has this iridescent purple and green ring around its neck. It just bobs its head and looks at me with that one orange eye. It never blinks. \n\nI wonder if birds ever wish they could disappear, or if they're just happy flying away whenever they want. But no... I haven't tried to hurt myself or anything. I just sit and watch the dust.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever thought about not wanting to be alive?\n\nPatient: Not wanting to be alive? No, I... I already said I don't think about dying. Dying is like... it's so final. Like when a TV turns off and there's just that black screen with the little white dot in the middle that shrinks down until it's gone. \n\nMy dad had this really old television in the garage. It was one of those big heavy ones with the wooden sides. He used to sit out there on a folding chair and watch baseball games. I don't understand baseball. There's just so much standing around and spitting in the dirt. But he liked the sound of it, the crack of the bat. Bats are made of ash wood, mostly. Or maple. Maple trees have those seeds that spin like helicopters when they fall. We used to call them whirlybirds when I was in elementary school. We'd gather them up in our pockets and throw them off the top of the slide to watch them spin. The slide was metal, and in the summer it would get so hot it would burn the back of your legs if you wore shorts. I always hated wearing shorts. \n\nBut yeah... I don't want to die. I just... I don't know. I just watch the dust.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever wished you could just disappear without actually wanting to do something to make that happen?\n\nPatient: Disappear... yeah, I said that before. I think about it sometimes. Just... fading out, you know? Like how the colors in a photograph fade if you leave it in the sun too long. My mom had this picture of her and my dad from their honeymoon in Florida, and it sat on the windowsill in the kitchen for years. \n\nFlorida has all those oranges. They sell them in those red mesh bags on the side of the highway. The mesh bags always remind me of the nets we used to use to catch minnows in the creek behind my house. We'd wade in with our rubber boots on, and the water was always freezing cold, even in July. Minnows are so fast, they just dart around like little silver flashes. You think you have one, and then you lift the net and there's just wet leaves and mud. Mud smells so specific, doesn't it? Earthy and kind of... metallic. \n\nBut I wouldn't do anything to make it happen. Fading away, I mean. I don't want to do anything. I just... think about the photograph getting lighter and lighter until it's just white paper.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that your thoughts are slower than they used to be?\n\nPatient: Slower? I... no, I haven't really noticed them being slow. They just kind of go. Sometimes I watch the clock in the kitchen, the one above the stove, and the second hand moves so jerky. Tick, tick, tick. It's not smooth at all. I tried to draw a perfect circle once, but my hand kept jerking like that clock. We had to draw circles in art class in high school. My teacher always smelled like peppermint and turpentine. Peppermints are almost too spicy, don't you think? The red and white stripes always remind me of those barber poles. There's a barber shop down the street from my house that still has one of those spinning poles outside. It just spins and spins, but the stripes never actually go anywhere. They just keep wrapping around themselves forever. I wonder who invented those.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your body moves more slowly than it used to?\n\nPatient: Moves slowly? No, I haven't really noticed my body doing that. I just walk the same. Walking is weird if you actually think about it too much, though, how you just fall forward and catch yourself over and over. I watched a caterpillar on the sidewalk yesterday and it moved so slow, just rippling its whole body along the concrete. It was one of those fuzzy orange and black ones. Woolly bears, I think they're called. People say you can tell how bad the winter is going to be by how wide the brown band is on their back. \n\nSnow is so quiet when it falls. It just blankets everything and mutes all the cars on the street. I used to try to catch snowflakes on my tongue when I was younger, but they just taste like cold air. Air doesn't really have a flavor unless you're near the ocean. The ocean is too loud, though. The waves just keep crashing and crashing and they never stop to take a breath. It makes my ears ring if I listen to it too long. But no, my body moves fine.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any change in how your clothes fit?\n\nPatient: My clothes? Um... no, I don't think so. They fit the same. I mostly just wear my green sweater anyway. It's getting those little fuzzballs all over the sleeves, you know? Pilling, I think it's called. I like to sit and pull them off one by one. It feels a lot like peeling a sunburn. I got a really bad sunburn at the community pool once when I was younger. The water there always smelled so strongly of chlorine and... pennies. \n\nPennies are practically worthless now, aren't they? I saw one on the sidewalk by the bus stop yesterday and it was tails up, so I definitely didn't pick it up. Bad luck. I wonder who decided that heads was good luck and tails was bad. It's just a piece of copper. Well, zinc mostly, I think. Zinc is in sunscreen. The thick white kind that never rubs in all the way. It makes people look like ghosts walking around on the beach. But yeah, my clothes are just the same.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like it takes you longer to think of words or respond to questions than it used to?\n\nPatient: Longer to think of words? I don't... I don't know if it takes longer. Sometimes the words are just there, like they're waiting in a line. But lines are always so boring to stand in. I stood in line at the grocery store for what felt like hours the other day just to buy a carton of milk. The lady in front of me had this huge cart full of canned soup. Mostly tomato soup. \n\nTomato soup always makes me think of grilled cheese sandwiches, but only if the cheese is really melted and gooey. If it's cold, it just tastes like plastic. Plastic is everywhere now, isn't it? Even in the ocean. I saw a documentary about sea turtles eating plastic bags because they think they're jellyfish. Jellyfish are so strange, they don't even have brains but they know how to sting you. I got stung by a bee once when I was little, right on the bottom of my foot. My foot swelled up so big it looked like a balloon. Balloons always pop eventually, though. It's kind of sad when you think about it. But words... no, I think the words come out fine.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're moving slower physically than you used to?\n\nPatient: Slower physically? No, I didn't think so. I mean, you just asked me that, didn't you? About moving slow? I still just walk the same. Walking is really just... it's just putting one foot in front of the other. Like those little wind-up toys. I had a little wind-up tin robot when I was a kid. You'd turn the key in its back and it would march across the kitchen table until it fell right off the edge. \n\nGravity is weird like that. It just pulls everything down, no matter what. Even apples. Isaac Newton and the apple, right? But what if the apple was rotten? Do rotten apples fall faster? They get all squishy and brown and the fruit flies come. Fruit flies are so annoying, they just hover around in these tiny little clouds. Clouds look so soft, but they're just water vapor. You can't even touch them. If you tried to sit on a cloud, you'd just fall right through. Like the robot off the table. But no, I don't feel like I'm moving slow.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your thoughts and movements are slowed down at the same time?\n\nPatient: Both at the same time? Um... no, I don't think they do that. My brain just goes, and my body just goes. It's not like they're tied together or anything. Like... like a three-legged race. Did you ever do those in middle school field days? You tie your leg to someone else's leg with a bandana and try to run, but you always end up falling in the grass. We used these bright red bandanas. Red is supposed to make bulls angry, but I read on the internet once that bulls are actually colorblind. They just get mad at the cape flapping around. I'd be mad too if someone was waving a big piece of fabric right in my face. Fabric stores always smell so dusty, like old curtains. My grandma used to have these heavy velvet curtains that blocked out all the light. It made her living room look like a cave. Bats live in caves. They sleep upside down, which looks like it would give you a terrible headache. The blood just rushing to your head all day. But no, my thoughts and my moving, they don't slow down together like that.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like it's an effort to initiate movements or actions?\n\nPatient: An effort? Yeah... mostly I just can't make myself do anything anymore. I just sit in my room all day with the exact same clothes on. I don't even knit or journal or go walking like I used to. I used to knit these really long scarves. The yarn was this chunky blue kind, but it was acrylic because real wool makes my neck break out in a rash. Sheep look so soft from far away, but their wool is actually really scratchy. And they have those weird rectangular pupils. I saw a picture of a sheep's eye on the internet once and it looked just like a goat's eye. Goats will eat anything, even tin cans, they say. Tin cans are so sharp when you open them. I cut my finger on a peach can once and it bled all over the kitchen counter. Blood is so bright red when it first comes out, almost like paint. Paint takes forever to dry if you put it on too thick. But yeah, when my mom comes in and asks what's wrong with me, I barely even have the energy to answer her. It's just... talking takes too much air. Air is invisible but you can feel it when the wind blows. The wind knocked down a huge oak tree in our neighborhood last year. It just snapped right in half like a dry little twig. Twigs are good for starting campfires, if you have matches. But I don't really do anything anymore.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any days recently where you felt more interested in doing things, even if it was just for a little while?\n\nPatient: More interested? No, not really. I don't think I've had any days like that. Everything just feels... flat. Like a deflated balloon. We had balloons at my 16th birthday party, those shiny foil ones that float for weeks. They get all crinkly when they start losing helium, and they just sort of hover near the ceiling fan. Ceiling fans always make me nervous, like one of the blades is just gonna fly off and hit someone in the head. I saw a video once where a fan fell right into a bowl of soup on a dinner table. Soup is good when it's cold outside, especially tomato soup with grilled cheese. The cheese has to be really melty though, like the American kind that comes in those little plastic wrappers. Plastic is so bad for the ocean. All the sea turtles get stuck in those soda can rings. Turtles live for such a long time, sometimes over a hundred years. Imagine being alive for a hundred years. You'd see so much stuff change. But no, I haven't really felt interested in doing anything.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're unable to experience any kind of pleasure, even from things that you used to enjoy just a little bit?\n\nPatient: Pleasure? Um... no. Not even a little bit. Like I was saying before, I mostly just can't make myself do anything anymore. I just sit in my room with the exact same clothes on. I don't knit or journal or go walking like I used to. Walking is supposed to be really good for your heart, like cardio. My gym teacher in high school always made us run the mile, and he'd stand there with a stopwatch yelling at us to go faster. Stopwatches have that really loud clicking sound when you press the button. It sounds like a bug snapping its wings. Cicadas make the loudest noise in the summer. They leave those empty brown shells stuck to the sides of trees. Trees have rings inside them that tell you how old they are. If you cut one open, you can count them. But I wouldn't want to cut down a tree, they give us oxygen. But yeah, I don't enjoy anything. When my mom comes in and asks what's wrong, I barely even have the energy to answer her. It's just... nothing feels good anymore.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your current state is the worst it has ever been?\n\nPatient: The worst? I don't know... I guess so. It's hard to tell what the worst is when everything just feels the same every day. Every day is just a Tuesday. Tuesday is such a weird word when you look at it for too long. T-u-e-s. It doesn't even sound like how it's spelled. English is a really confusing language, I feel bad for people who have to learn it as a second language. My Spanish teacher in high school, Señora Martinez, she told us that Spanish is phonetic so you just say it how it looks. She had this really bright red lipstick she wore every single day. It matched her car, she drove this tiny little red convertible. Convertibles seem so dangerous if it rains suddenly. You'd get completely soaked before you could get the top back up. Rain always smells so specific, like wet dirt and pavement. Pavement gets so hot in the summer you could fry an egg on it. I saw a cartoon where someone did that once, but I don't think it would actually taste very good. The yolk would probably get gravel in it. Gravel driveways are so loud when you drive on them, they go crunch crunch crunch. But yeah... I don't really know if it's the worst. It's just bad.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"

Earthy and kind of... metallic. \n\nBut I wouldn't do anything to make it happen. Fading away, I mean. I don't want to do anything. I just... think about the photograph getting lighter and lighter until it's just white paper.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that your thoughts are slower than they used to be?\n\nPatient: Slower? I... no, I haven't really noticed them being slow. They just kind of go. Sometimes I watch the clock in the kitchen, the one above the stove, and the second hand moves so jerky. Tick, tick, tick. It's not smooth at all. I tried to draw a perfect circle once, but my hand kept jerking like that clock. We had to draw circles in art class in high school. My teacher always smelled like peppermint and turpentine. Peppermints are almost too spicy, don't you think? The red and white stripes always remind me of those barber poles. There's a barber shop down the street from my house that still has one of those spinning poles outside. It just spins and spins, but the stripes never actually go anywhere. They just keep wrapping around themselves forever. I wonder who invented those.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your body moves more slowly than it used to?\n\nPatient: Moves slowly? No, I haven't really noticed my body doing that. I just walk the same. Walking is weird if you actually think about it too much, though, how you just fall forward and catch yourself over and over. I watched a caterpillar on the sidewalk yesterday and it moved so slow, just rippling its whole body along the concrete. It was one of those fuzzy orange and black ones. Woolly bears, I think they're called. People say you can tell how bad the winter is going to be by how wide the brown band is on their back. \n\nSnow is so quiet when it falls. It just blankets everything and mutes all the cars on the street. I used to try to catch snowflakes on my tongue when I was younger, but they just taste like cold air. Air doesn't really have a flavor unless you're near the ocean. The ocean is too loud, though. The waves just keep crashing and crashing and they never stop to take a breath. It makes my ears ring if I listen to it too long. But no, my body moves fine.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any change in how your clothes fit?\n\nPatient: My clothes? Um... no, I don't think so. They fit the same. I mostly just wear my green sweater anyway. It's getting those little fuzzballs all over the sleeves, you know? Pilling, I think it's called. I like to sit and pull them off one by one. It feels a lot like peeling a sunburn. I got a really bad sunburn at the community pool once when I was younger. The water there always smelled so strongly of chlorine and... pennies. \n\nPennies are practically worthless now, aren't they? I saw one on the sidewalk by the bus stop yesterday and it was tails up, so I definitely didn't pick it up. Bad luck. I wonder who decided that heads was good luck and tails was bad. It's just a piece of copper. Well, zinc mostly, I think. Zinc is in sunscreen. The thick white kind that never rubs in all the way. It makes people look like ghosts walking around on the beach. But yeah, my clothes are just the same.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like it takes you longer to think of words or respond to questions than it used to?\n\nPatient: Longer to think of words? I don't... I don't know if it takes longer. Sometimes the words are just there, like they're waiting in a line. But lines are always so boring to stand in. I stood in line at the grocery store for what felt like hours the other day just to buy a carton of milk. The lady in front of me had this huge cart full of canned soup. Mostly tomato soup. \n\nTomato soup always makes me think of grilled cheese sandwiches, but only if the cheese is really melted and gooey. If it's cold, it just tastes like plastic. Plastic is everywhere now, isn't it? Even in the ocean. I saw a documentary about sea turtles eating plastic bags because they think they're jellyfish. Jellyfish are so strange, they don't even have brains but they know how to sting you. I got stung by a bee once when I was little, right on the bottom of my foot. My foot swelled up so big it looked like a balloon. Balloons always pop eventually, though. It's kind of sad when you think about it. But words... no, I think the words come out fine.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're moving slower physically than you used to?\n\nPatient: Slower physically? No, I didn't think so. I mean, you just asked me that, didn't you? About moving slow? I still just walk the same. Walking is really just... it's just putting one foot in front of the other. Like those little wind-up toys. I had a little wind-up tin robot when I was a kid. You'd turn the key in its back and it would march across the kitchen table until it fell right off the edge. \n\nGravity is weird like that. It just pulls everything down, no matter what. Even apples. Isaac Newton and the apple, right? But what if the apple was rotten? Do rotten apples fall faster? They get all squishy and brown and the fruit flies come. Fruit flies are so annoying, they just hover around in these tiny little clouds. Clouds look so soft, but they're just water vapor. You can't even touch them. If you tried to sit on a cloud, you'd just fall right through. Like the robot off the table. But no, I don't feel like I'm moving slow.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your thoughts and movements are slowed down at the same time?\n\nPatient: Both at the same time? Um... no, I don't think they do that. My brain just goes, and my body just goes. It's not like they're tied together or anything. Like... like a three-legged race. Did you ever do those in middle school field days? You tie your leg to someone else's leg with a bandana and try to run, but you always end up falling in the grass. We used these bright red bandanas. Red is supposed to make bulls angry, but I read on the internet once that bulls are actually colorblind. They just get mad at the cape flapping around. I'd be mad too if someone was waving a big piece of fabric right in my face. Fabric stores always smell so dusty, like old curtains. My grandma used to have these heavy velvet curtains that blocked out all the light. It made her living room look like a cave. Bats live in caves. They sleep upside down, which looks like it would give you a terrible headache. The blood just rushing to your head all day. But no, my thoughts and my moving, they don't slow down together like that.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like it's an effort to initiate movements or actions?\n\nPatient: An effort? Yeah... mostly I just can't make myself do anything anymore. I just sit in my room all day with the exact same clothes on. I don't even knit or journal or go walking like I used to. I used to knit these really long scarves. The yarn was this chunky blue kind, but it was acrylic because real wool makes my neck break out in a rash. Sheep look so soft from far away, but their wool is actually really scratchy. And they have those weird rectangular pupils. I saw a picture of a sheep's eye on the internet once and it looked just like a goat's eye. Goats will eat anything, even tin cans, they say. Tin cans are so sharp when you open them. I cut my finger on a peach can once and it bled all over the kitchen counter. Blood is so bright red when it first comes out, almost like paint. Paint takes forever to dry if you put it on too thick. But yeah, when my mom comes in and asks what's wrong with me, I barely even have the energy to answer her. It's just... talking takes too much air. Air is invisible but you can feel it when the wind blows. The wind knocked down a huge oak tree in our neighborhood last year. It just snapped right in half like a dry little twig. Twigs are good for starting campfires, if you have matches. But I don't really do anything anymore.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any days recently where you felt more interested in doing things, even if it was just for a little while?\n\nPatient: More interested? No, not really. I don't think I've had any days like that. Everything just feels... flat. Like a deflated balloon. We had balloons at my 16th birthday party, those shiny foil ones that float for weeks. They get all crinkly when they start losing helium, and they just sort of hover near the ceiling fan. Ceiling fans always make me nervous, like one of the blades is just gonna fly off and hit someone in the head. I saw a video once where a fan fell right into a bowl of soup on a dinner table. Soup is good when it's cold outside, especially tomato soup with grilled cheese. The cheese has to be really melty though, like the American kind that comes in those little plastic wrappers. Plastic is so bad for the ocean. All the sea turtles get stuck in those soda can rings. Turtles live for such a long time, sometimes over a hundred years. Imagine being alive for a hundred years. You'd see so much stuff change. But no, I haven't really felt interested in doing anything.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're unable to experience any kind of pleasure, even from things that you used to enjoy just a little bit?\n\nPatient: Pleasure? Um... no. Not even a little bit. Like I was saying before, I mostly just can't make myself do anything anymore. I just sit in my room with the exact same clothes on. I don't knit or journal or go walking like I used to. Walking is supposed to be really good for your heart, like cardio. My gym teacher in high school always made us run the mile, and he'd stand there with a stopwatch yelling at us to go faster. Stopwatches have that really loud clicking sound when you press the button. It sounds like a bug snapping its wings. Cicadas make the loudest noise in the summer. They leave those empty brown shells stuck to the sides of trees. Trees have rings inside them that tell you how old they are. If you cut one open, you can count them. But I wouldn't want to cut down a tree, they give us oxygen. But yeah, I don't enjoy anything. When my mom comes in and asks what's wrong, I barely even have the energy to answer her. It's just... nothing feels good anymore.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your current state is the worst it has ever been?\n\nPatient: The worst? I don't know... I guess so. It's hard to tell what the worst is when everything just feels the same every day. Every day is just a Tuesday. Tuesday is such a weird word when you look at it for too long. T-u-e-s. It doesn't even sound like how it's spelled. English is a really confusing language, I feel bad for people who have to learn it as a second language. My Spanish teacher in high school, Señora Martinez, she told us that Spanish is phonetic so you just say it how it looks. She had this really bright red lipstick she wore every single day. It matched her car, she drove this tiny little red convertible. Convertibles seem so dangerous if it rains suddenly. You'd get completely soaked before you could get the top back up. Rain always smells so specific, like wet dirt and pavement. Pavement gets so hot in the summer you could fry an egg on it. I saw a cartoon where someone did that once, but I don't think it would actually taste very good. The yolk would probably get gravel in it. Gravel driveways are so loud when you drive on them, they go crunch crunch crunch. But yeah... I don't really know if it's the worst. It's just bad.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Like slugs. I poured salt on a slug once when I was little and it just... dissolved. It was really awful. I felt bad for days. But I don't really feel bad like that anymore.
